Such prior leasehold agreements shall be duly recorded prior to August 1, 1978. Prior to the issuance of any permit for a development or enlargement pursuant to this Resolution a complete metes and bounds of the zoning lot, the tax lot number, the block number and the ownership of the zoning lot as set forth in paragraphs (a), (b), (c) and (d) herein shall be recorded by the applicant in the Conveyances Section of the Office of the City Register (or, if applicable, the County Clerk's Office) of the county in which the said zoning lot is located. ### Permitext answer

No. On the stated facts, the lots cannot be combined as a new zoning lot merely through common ownership. ZR 12-10’s current single-fee-ownership route requires two or more lots of record within one block to be contiguous for **a minimum of 10 linear feet** at the applicable filing time, and they touch for only eight feet.

The declaration route also requires the lots to be contiguous for a minimum of 10 linear feet. Thus, recording a Declaration of Restrictions cannot cure the eight-foot shortfall. The historical single-ownership route likewise does not apply on the stated premise that the lots were not historically one zoning lot: it applies only where the contiguous tract was in single ownership on December 15, 1961, or an applicable subsequent amendment date.

This answer addresses the general ZR 12-10 definition only. The supplied evidence does not establish the properties’ mapped district or any special-district rule that could bear on a particular filing.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Ten-foot contiguity is required for a newly combined zoning lot: Under ZR 12-10(c), a tract of two or more lots of record in one block may be a zoning lot at the permit filing (or, if no permit is required, certificate-of-occupancy filing) only if it is under single fee ownership, all required parties are parties in interest, and the lots are contiguous for a minimum of 10 linear feet. An eight-linear-foot connection does not satisfy that express minimum.
- A declaration does not eliminate the ten-foot minimum: ZR 12-10(d) permits a tract to be declared and treated as one zoning lot only where its lots are contiguous for a minimum of 10 linear feet. It also requires the applicable written Declaration of Restrictions, execution by each party in interest unless validly waived, and recording against each affected lot. Because the stated connection is eight feet, this route is unavailable regardless of common ownership or a proposed declaration.
- Historical single ownership is a separate definition route: ZR 12-10(b) covers a contiguous tract in one block that was in single ownership on December 15, 1961, or an applicable subsequent amendment date. The stated fact that the lots were not historically one zoning lot means that this historical route is not established on the supplied facts; it does not allow current common ownership alone to overcome the current ten-foot requirement.

**ZR 12-10 — canonical section 20018523**

> cellar General Definition Last Amended 10/25/1993 A "cellar," except where a base plane is used to determine building height, is a space wholly or partly below curb level, with more than one-half its height (measured from floor to ceiling) below curb level. On through lots, the curb level nearest to such space shall be used to determine whether such space is a cellar. Where a base plane is used to determine building height, a cellar is a space wholly or partly below the base plane, with more than one-half its height (measured from floor to ceiling) below the base plane. In addition, the following rules shall apply: (a) When a sloping base plane is established, a cellar is a space wholly or partly below the street wall line level, with more than one-half its height (measured from floor to ceiling) below the street wall line level used to establish such base plane. On through lots, the street wall line level nearest to such space shall be used to determine whether such space is a cellar. (b) All of the floor space with at least one-half its height (measured from floor to ceiling) above curb level shall be considered to be a basement where, subsequent to December 5, 1990, the level of any yard except that portion of a yard in front of the entrance to a garage on a zoning lot is lowered below the level of the base plane.
> floor area General Definition Last Amended 12/5/2024 “Floor area” is the sum of the gross areas of the several floors of a building or buildings, measured from the exterior faces of exterior walls or from the center lines of walls separating two buildings. In particular, floor area includes: basement space, except as specifically excluded in this definition; elevator shafts or stairwells at each floor, except as specifically excluded in this definition; floor space in penthouses; attic space (whether or not a floor has been laid) providing structural headroom of eight feet or more; floor space in gallerias, interior balconies, mezzanines or bridges; floor space in open or roofed bridges, breeze ways or porches, if more than 50 percent of the perimeter of such bridge, breeze way or porch is enclosed, and provided that a parapet not higher than 3 feet, 8 inches, or a railing not less than 50 percent open and not higher than 4 feet, 6 inches, shall not constitute an enclosure; any other floor space used for dwelling purposes, no matter where located within a building, when not specifically excluded; floor space in accessory buildings, except for floor space used for accessory mechanical equipment; floor space used for accessory off-street loading berths in excess of 200 percent of the amount required by the applicable district regulations; floor space that is not otherwise exempt pursuant to this Section and is, or is made, inaccessible within a building; floor space in exterior balconies or in open or roofed terraces if more than 67 percent of the perimeter of such balcony or terrace is enclosed and provided that parapets, railings or safety guards, whether applied singly, or in combination, shall not constitute an enclosure when meeting the following criteria: parapets shall not exceed four feet in height; railings shall not exceed 4 feet, 6 inches, and shall be at least 50 percent open for the portion that exceeds four feet in height; and safety guards shall not exceed 10 feet in height and shall be at least 90 percent transparent for the portion that exceeds four feet in height. In addition, where such balcony or terrace has a roofed portion above it, there shall be an opening that is not less than 40 percent of the height between the bottom of the roof and the finished floor level of such balcony. For the purposes of such calculation, exterior building walls on adjoining zoning lots abutting an open or roofed terrace shall not constitute an enclosure. A sun control device that is accessible for purposes other than for maintenance shall be considered a balcony; and any other floor space not specifically excluded. However, the floor area of a building shall not include: cellar space, except where such space is used for dwelling purposes. Cellar space used for retailing shall be included for the purpose of calculating requirements for accessory off-street parking spaces, accessory bicycle parking spaces and accessory off-street loading berths; elevator or stair bulkheads, accessory water tanks, or cooling towers, except that such exclusions shall not apply in R2A Districts; uncovered steps; attic space (whether or not a floor has been laid) providing structural headroom of less than eight feet; floor space in open or roofed bridges, breeze ways or porches, provided that not more than 50 percent of the perimeter of such bridge, breeze way or porch is enclosed, and provided that a parapet not higher than 3 feet, 8 inches, or a railing not less than 50 percent open and not higher than 4 feet, 6 inches, shall not constitute an enclosure; floor space used for accessory off-street parking spaces provided in any story: up to 300 square feet per single- or two-family residence, on zoning lots where individual parking spaces accessory to residential uses are provided; within group parking facilities located not more than 23 feet above curb level, except where such floor space used for accessory parking is contained within a public parking garage; or within automated parking facilities located not more than 40 feet above curb level, except where such floor space used for accessory parking is contained within a public parking garage; floor space used for accessory off-street loading berths, up to 200 percent of the amount required by the applicable district regulation; floor space used for accessory mechanical equipment. Such exclusion shall also include the minimum necessary floor space to provide for necessary maintenance and access to such equipment. For the purposes of calculating floor space used for mechanical equipment, building segments on a single zoning lot may be considered to be separate buildings; floor space in exterior balconies or in open or roofed terraces provided that not more than 67 percent of the perimeter of such balcony or terrace is enclosed and provided that parapets, railings or safety guards, whether applied singly or in combination, shall not constitute an enclosure when meeting the following criteria: parapets shall not exceed four feet in height; railings shall not exceed 4 feet, 6 inches, and shall be at least 50 percent open for the portion that exceeds four feet in height; and safety guards shall not exceed 10 feet in height and shall be at least 90 percent transparent for the portion that exceeds four feet in height. In addition, where such balcony or terrace has a roofed portion above it, there shall be an opening that is not less than 40 percent of the height between the bottom of the roof and the finished floor level of such balcony, For the purposes of such calculation, exterior building walls on adjoining zoning lots abutting an open or roofed terrace shall not constitute an enclosure. A sun control device that is accessible for purposes other than for maintenance shall be considered a balcony; floor space within stairwells: at each floor of buildings containing residences developed or enlarged after April 16, 2008, that are greater than 125 feet in height, provided that: such stairwells are located on a story containing residences; such stairwells are used as a required means of egress from such residences; such stairwells have a minimum width of 44 inches; such floor space excluded from floor area shall be limited to a maximum of eight inches of stair and landing width measured along the length of the stairwell enclosure at each floor; and where such stairwells serve non-residential uses on any floor, or are located within multi-level dwelling units, the entire floor space within such stairwells on such floors shall count as floor area; at each floor of buildings developed or enlarged after April 28, 2015, that are 420 feet or greater in height, provided that: such stairwells serve a space with an occupancy group other than Group R-2, as classified in the New York City Building Code, that is located at or above a height of 420 feet; and such floor space excluded from floor area shall be limited to: the 25 percent of stair and landing width required by the New York City Building Code which is provided in addition to the stair and landing widths required by such Code for means of egress; or the one stairwell required by the New York City Building Code which is provided in addition to the stairwells required by such Code for means of egress. For the purposes of this paragraph, such additional stairwell shall include the stair and landings as well as any walls enclosing the stair and landings; floor space used for the storage of equipment by the Fire Department pursuant to the New York City Fire Code, Section 511.7 (Storage space for pre-positioned department equipment) in buildings that are 420 feet or greater in height; qualifying exterior wall thickness; floor space in a qualifying rooftop greenhouse; floor space on a sun control device, where such space is inaccessible other than for maintenance; floor space within a fully electrified building or an ultra low energy building, of an amount equivalent to five percent of the floor area located within such building, and exclusive of any floor space otherwise excluded from floor area; floor space in buildings containing multiple dwelling residences allocated to building amenities, corridors, refuse storage or disposal, or access to elevated ground floor dwelling units that is provided in accordance with the provisions of Section 23-23, inclusive; floor space in Quality Housing buildings that was exempted pursuant the Quality Housing Program, as such program existed prior to December 5, 2024.

### Permitext answer

Yes. On the stated facts, the level is a cellar: where a base plane is used to determine building height, a space wholly or partly below the base plane with more than one-half of its floor-to-ceiling height below it is a cellar. ZR 12-10 excludes cellar space from zoning floor area except when it is used for dwelling purposes. Because the 5,000-square-foot level is used for non-dwelling storage, its 5,000 square feet does not count as zoning floor area.

The square footage does not alter that result. The definition gives cellar space used for retailing a limited role in calculating accessory off-street parking, bicycle parking, and loading requirements; that is not a general inclusion in zoning floor area and does not apply to the stated storage use.

The definition has distinct measurement rules if the base plane is sloping: use the street-wall-line level used to establish that base plane, and on a through lot use the nearest such street-wall-line level. Its curb-level and post–December 5, 1990 lowered-yard provisions govern other stated definitional circumstances, not the base-plane facts given here.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- The stated below-grade level is a cellar: Where a base plane is used to determine building height, a cellar is a space wholly or partly below the base plane with more than one-half of its floor-to-ceiling height below the base plane. For a sloping base plane, the reference is the street-wall-line level used to establish that base plane; on through lots, the nearest street-wall-line level controls. The passage separately uses curb level where a base plane is not used to determine building height, and provides a basement treatment for specified post–December 5, 1990 yard lowering.
- Non-dwelling cellar space is excluded from zoning floor area: Although floor area generally consists of gross floor areas, cellar space is expressly excluded except where used for dwelling purposes. The separate rule for cellar space used for retailing is limited to accessory off-street parking, bicycle-parking, and loading-berth calculations; it does not make retail cellar space general zoning floor area.

## 21. Nonconforming Use Discontinuance

**Result:** PASS — 4.00/4.00

**Case ID:** zr-nonconforming-use-discontinuance

**Jurisdiction / edition:** New York City, New York; NYC Zoning Resolution — text through 2026-08-13

**Project context:** `{}`

**Question:** A building contains a lawful non-conforming use, but active operation of substantially all of that use stopped for 30 continuous months even though the owner always intended to reopen it. Can the owner simply restart the use?

### Selected evidence

**ZR 52-61 — canonical section 20018658**

> If, for a continuous period of two years, either the non-conforming use of land with minor improvements is discontinued, or the active operation of substantially all the non-conforming uses in any building or other structure is discontinued, such land or building or other structure shall thereafter be used only for a conforming use. Intent to resume active operations shall not affect the foregoing.

The provisions of this Section shall not apply where such discontinuance of active operations is directly caused by war, strikes or other labor difficulties, a governmental program of materials rationing, or the construction of a duly authorized improvement project by a governmental body or a public utility company.

However, the provisions of this Section shall also not apply to vacant ground floor or basement stores in buildings designed for residential use located in R1 through R12 Districts, where the changed or reactivated use is a use listed in Use Group VI that would be permitted in a C1 District that is a select commercial overlay, or an office, as listed in Use Group VII.

Furthermore, the provisions of this Section shall be modified to allow transient hotels, motels or tourists cabins existing on December 9, 2021, in a Residence District, to be restored to such use until December 9, 2027, regardless of more than two years of discontinuance of the use, and regardless of any change of use between December 9, 2021, and December 9, 2027.

In addition, the changed or reactivated use shall be subject to the provisions of Section 52-34 (Commercial Uses in Residence Districts).

### Permitext answer

No—on the stated facts, the owner cannot simply restart the non-conforming use. ZR 52-61 provides that when the active operation of substantially all the non-conforming uses in any building or other structure is discontinued for a continuous period of two years, the building or structure shall thereafter be used only for a conforming use. Thirty continuous months exceeds two years, and the owner’s continuing intent to reopen does not alter that result because the provision expressly states: Intent to resume active operations shall not affect the foregoing.

That conclusion is subject only to the specific qualifications in the supplied passage: the two-year discontinuance rule does not apply when the discontinuance was directly caused by war, strikes or other labor difficulties, a governmental materials-rationing program, or construction of a duly authorized governmental or public-utility improvement project. The passage also provides distinct reactivation rules for certain vacant ground-floor or basement stores in residentially designed buildings in R1 through R12 Districts, and for specified transient lodging uses in Residence Districts through December 9, 2027. No facts provided establish any of those exceptions or modified-use provisions. Where either reactivation provision applies, the changed or reactivated use is also subject to ZR 52-34; that section was not supplied.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Two-year discontinuance ends the non-conforming-use right: ZR 52-61 states that if active operation of substantially all non-conforming uses in a building or other structure is discontinued continuously for two years, it shall thereafter be used only for a conforming use. A stated 30-month discontinuance exceeds that period.
- Intent to reopen does not preserve the use: The provision expressly states: Intent to resume active operations shall not affect the foregoing. Thus, the owner’s stated intent to reopen does not prevent the consequence of a qualifying two-year discontinuance.
- Specified exceptions and reactivation provisions are limited: The two-year rule does not apply if discontinuance was directly caused by war, strikes or other labor difficulties, governmental materials rationing, or a duly authorized governmental or public-utility improvement project. The passage separately addresses certain vacant stores in residentially designed R1 through R12 buildings and specified transient lodging uses in Residence Districts through December 9, 2027; it makes any changed or reactivated use under those provisions subject to ZR 52-34.

- None

Section 52-61 generally requires the building thereafter to be used only for a conforming use after active operation of substantially all non-conforming uses is discontinued continuously for two years. The owner's intent to resume active operations does not preserve the use under the general rule. Thirty continuous months exceeds the section's two-year period. **Case ID:** zr-candidate-b1-r6a-uap-insufficient-affordable-area

**Jurisdiction / edition:** New York City, New York; NYC Zoning Resolution — text through 2026-08-13

**Project context:** `{}`

**Question:** My 8,000-square-foot R6A lot is outside an MIH area. I want 31,000 square feet of residential floor area, including 6,200 square feet that I intend to make affordable. Since 31,000 square feet is below the R6A 3.90 FAR limit, can I build it?

### Selected evidence

**ZR 23-22 — canonical section 20018017**

> R6 R7 R8 R9 R10 R11 R12

In the districts indicated, the maximum residential floor area ratio shall be as set forth in the following table. Separate maximum residential floor area ratios are set forth for zoning lots containing standard residences and zoning lots containing qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing.

MAXIMUM FLOOR AREA RATIO FOR R6-R12 DISTRICTS

District Standard residences Qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing R6A R61 R6-1 R7B 3.00 3.90 R6 2.20 3.90 R6B 2.00 2.40 R6D R6-2 2.50 3.00 R7A R7-11 R7-21 4.00 5.01 R7-1 R7-2 3.44 5.01 R7D 4.66 5.60 R7X R7-3 5.00 6.00 R8A R8X R8 6.02 7.20 R8 7.201 8.642 R8B 4.00 4.80 R9A R9 7.52 9.02 R9D R9X R9-1 9.00 10.80 R10A R10X R10 10.00 12.00 R11 12.00 15.00 R12 15.00 18.00

1 For zoning lots, or portions thereof, located within 100 feet of a wide street

2 Outside of Mandatory Inclusionary Housing areas, for zoning lots, or portions thereof, located within 100 feet of a wide street, containing UAP developments or qualifying senior housing

**ZR 27-111 — canonical section 20022699**

> The following definitions shall apply throughout Section 27-10 (ADMINISTRATION OF AFFORDABLE HOUSING), inclusive:

Affordable floor area

Where all of the dwelling units, rooming units and supportive housing units in an MIH site or UAP site, other than any super’s unit, are affordable housing units, all of the residential floor area or community facility floor area for a supportive housing project, in such UAP site or MIH site is “affordable floor area.” Where one or more of the dwelling units or rooming units in an MIH site or UAP site, other than any super’s unit, are not affordable housing units, the affordable floor area in such MIH site or UAP site is the sum of: all of the residential floor area of the affordable housing units in such MIH site or UAP site; plus a figure determined by multiplying the residential floor area of the eligible common areas in such MIH site or UAP site by a fraction, the numerator of which is all of the residential floor area of the affordable housing units in such MIH site or UAP site and the denominator of which is the sum of the residential floor area of the affordable housing units in such MIH site or UAP site plus the residential floor area of the dwelling units or rooming units in such MIH site or UAP site, other than any super’s unit, that are not affordable housing units.

Affordable housing

“Affordable housing” consists of:

affordable housing units; and eligible common areas.

Affordable housing application

An “affordable housing application” is an application submitted to HPD that specifies how affordable housing will be provided on an MIH site or a UAP site, in compliance with the provisions of Section 27-00 (APPLICABILITY, GENERAL PURPOSES AND DEFINITIONS), inclusive.

Affordable housing fund

With respect to the requirements of paragraph (a)(3)(v) of Section 27-131, the “affordable housing fund” is a fund administered by HPD, all contributions to which shall be used for development, acquisition, rehabilitation, or preservation of affordable housing, or other affordable housing purposes as set forth in the guidelines. Each contribution into such fund shall be reserved for use within the borough in which the MIH development making such contribution is located and, for a minimum of 10 years, shall be reserved for use in the same Community District in which the MIH development making such contribution is located. HPD shall issue a public report on the use of such fund no less frequently than annually.

Further provisions for the use of such funds may be set forth in the guidelines.

Affordable housing regulatory agreement

An “affordable housing regulatory agreement” is a legally binding agreement between the owner of a building and a Federal, State, or local agency or instrumentality with respect to a development, enlargement, or conversion from non-residential to residential use, which:

requires all of the dwelling units, rooming units, or supportive housing units in such building to be income-restricted and occupied by an eligible household as established by such agreement for a period of no less than 30 years; for a rental building, restricts an amount of floor area that would otherwise be required for the MIH development, UAP development or qualifying residential site subject to affordability requirements for the life of such building, or, for a homeownership building, requires such building to be owned by a housing development fund corporation established pursuant to Article XI of the Private Housing Finance Law for the life of such building; and is entered into in connection with public funding.

HPD may impose additional requirements for buildings subject to an affordable housing regulatory agreement in the guidelines.

Affordable housing unit

An “affordable housing unit” is:

a dwelling unit, other than a super’s unit, that is used for class A occupancy as defined in the Multiple Dwelling Law, or a rooming unit, other than a super’s unit, that is used for either Class A or Class B occupancy as defined in the Multiple Dwelling Law, and that is or will be restricted, pursuant to an affordable housing regulatory agreement or restrictive declaration, to occupancy by: for a UAP site: households having an income less than or equal to a weighted average of 60 percent of the income index at initial occupancy: with no more than three income bands; no income band exceeding 100 percent of the income index; and for UAP sites with 10,000 square feet or more of affordable floor area, at least 20 percent of such affordable floor area at an income band of no more than 40 percent of the income index. However, with regard to preservation affordable housing, a grandfathered tenant shall also be permitted to occupy such affordable housing unit; or households as specified in an affordable housing regulatory agreement executed after December 5, 2024; or for an MIH site, qualifying households; a supportive housing unit within a supportive housing project.

Affordable housing units that are restricted to homeownership, as defined in Section 27-113, pursuant to an affordable housing regulatory agreement or a restrictive declaration, must be dwelling units.

Capital element

“Capital elements” are, with respect to any UAP site, the electrical, plumbing, heating and ventilation systems in such UAP site, any air conditioning system in such UAP site and all facades, parapets, roofs, windows, doors, elevators, concrete and masonry in such UAP site and any other portions of such UAP site specified in the guidelines.

Completion notice

A “completion notice” is a notice from HPD to the Department of Buildings stating that the affordable housing in all or a portion of any MIH site or UAP site is complete and stating the affordable floor area of such affordable housing.

Eligible common area

An eligible common area includes any residential floor area that is located within a super’s unit, and any residential floor area in such MIH site or UAP site that is not located within any other dwelling unit or rooming unit, but shall not include any residential floor area for which a user fee is charged to residents of affordable housing units.

Grandfathered tenant

A “grandfathered tenant” is any household that:

occupied an affordable housing unit in preservation affordable housing on the restrictive declaration date, pursuant to a lease, occupancy agreement or statutory tenancy under which one or more members of such household was a primary tenant of such affordable housing unit; and has not been certified to have an annual income below the income band applicable to such affordable housing unit; or in homeownership preservation affordable housing, has been certified to have an annual income below the income band applicable to such affordable housing unit, but has elected not to purchase such affordable housing unit.

In Mandatory Inclusionary Housing areas, grandfathered tenants may include tenants of buildings on an MIH site that have been or will be demolished, as set forth in the guidelines.

**ZR 27-151 — canonical section 20022710**

> MIH development or UAP development building permits# HPD may issue a permit notice to the Department of Buildings at any time on or after the restrictive declaration date. The Department of Buildings may thereafter issue building permits to an MIH development or a UAP development based on the affordable housing or, in the case of an MIH development, contribution to the affordable housing fund described in such permit notice#. If HPD does not receive confirmation that the restrictive declaration has been recorded within 45 days after the later of the restrictive declaration date or the date upon which HPD authorizes the recording of the restrictive declaration, HPD shall suspend or revoke such permit notice, notify the Department of Buildings of such suspension or revocation and not reinstate such permit notice or issue any new permit notice until HPD receives confirmation that the restrictive declaration has been recorded or any applicable alternate procedure has been completed. Upon receipt of notice from HPD that a permit notice has been suspended or revoked, the Department of Buildings shall suspend or revoke each building permit issued pursuant to such permit notice which is then in effect for any MIH development or UAP development. MIH development or UAP development certificates of occupancy The Department of Buildings shall not issue a permanent certificate of occupancy for any MIH development or UAP development until HPD has issued a completion notice with respect to the affordable housing that satisfies the requirements of this Chapter. However, where any story of an MIH development or UAP development contains one or more affordable housing units, the Department of Buildings may issue a temporary certificate of occupancy for such story if such temporary certificate of occupancy either includes each affordable housing unit located in such story or only includes dwelling units or rooming units that are affordable housing units. Nothing in the preceding sentence shall be deemed to prohibit the granting of a temporary certificate of occupancy for the standard residential floor area in a UAP development or the granting of a temporary or permanent certificate of occupancy for a super’s unit. HPD shall not issue a completion notice with respect to any portion of any MIH site or UAP site unless: the Department of Buildings has issued temporary certificates of occupancy for all affordable housing described in such completion notice and such certificates of occupancy have not expired, been suspended or been revoked; or where a UAP site contains affordable housing that had a valid certificate of occupancy on the restrictive declaration date and no new temporary or permanent certificate of occupancy is thereafter required for the creation of such affordable housing, HPD has determined that all renovation and repair work required by the applicable restrictive declaration has been completed and all obligations with respect to the creation of such affordable housing have been fulfilled in accordance with the applicable restrictive declaration. UAP developments and MIH developments that are restricted pursuant to an affordable housing regulatory agreement shall not be required to comply with this Section.

### Permitext answer

No—not on the supplied facts. For an R6A zoning lot with standard residences, the maximum residential FAR is 3.00. The calculation is 8,000 square feet × 3.00 = 24,000 square feet. Your proposed 31,000 square feet is 31,000 ÷ 8,000 = 3.875 FAR, exceeding that limit by 7,000 square feet.

ZR 23-22 separately lists 3.90 FAR for an R6A zoning lot containing qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing. Numerically, that cap would allow 8,000 × 3.90 = 31,200 square feet, so the proposed 31,000 square feet is below it. But allocating 6,200 square feet to units you intend to make affordable does not itself establish that the project contains qualifying affordable housing for the 3.90-FAR category. The supplied provisions do not include the eligibility provision that establishes that status outside an MIH area.

The stated 6,200 square feet also cannot necessarily be treated as 6,200 square feet of defined affordable floor area. For a mixed MIH or UAP site, affordable floor area is the affordable-unit residential area plus a proportionate share of eligible common-area floor area; it is not necessarily just the unit area designated affordable. The supplied UAP definition requires affordable-unit restrictions through an affordable housing regulatory agreement or restrictive declaration, with the specified UAP income restrictions. The additional UAP rule requiring at least 20 percent of affordable floor area at no more than 40 percent of the income index applies only if the UAP site has 10,000 square feet or more of affordable floor area; it is not triggered by the stated 6,200 square feet alone.

If the project proceeds as a UAP development, HPD may issue a permit notice on or after the restrictive-declaration date, after which DOB may issue permits based on the affordable housing described in that notice. If HPD does not receive confirmation that the restrictive declaration was recorded within 45 days after the later of the restrictive-declaration date or HPD’s authorization of recording, HPD must suspend or revoke the notice; DOB must then suspend or revoke permits issued pursuant to that notice. A permanent certificate of occupancy generally cannot issue until HPD issues a completion notice. HPD may issue that notice only after DOB has issued unexpired, unsuspended, and unrevoked temporary certificates of occupancy for all affordable housing covered by the notice, or, for the specified existing-UAP-housing alternative, after HPD determines that required renovation and repair work and all creation obligations under the restrictive declaration have been completed. The selected provision exempts UAP and MIH developments restricted under an affordable housing regulatory agreement from these procedures.

So, 31,000 square feet is not permitted under the directly applicable standard-residence limit. It could fit within the separate 3.90-FAR ceiling only if the project satisfies the unsupplied rules that make it a qualifying-affordable-housing project; a 20-percent affordability intention alone does not prove that entitlement.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- R6A has separate FAR caps: ZR 23-22 establishes separate maximum residential FARs for standard residences and for zoning lots containing qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing. The R6A row provides 3.00 FAR for standard residences and 3.90 FAR for qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing.
- The proposal exceeds the standard-residence cap: On the stated 8,000-square-foot lot, 3.00 FAR permits 24,000 square feet. The proposed 31,000 square feet is 3.875 FAR and exceeds that cap by 7,000 square feet. The separate 3.90-FAR category permits 31,200 square feet on the stated lot, but that arithmetic does not establish entitlement to the qualifying-affordable-housing or qualifying-senior-housing category.
- Affordable floor area and affordable units have defined MIH/UAP conditions: For a mixed MIH or UAP site, affordable floor area includes the affordable-unit residential floor area plus a proportionate share of eligible common areas. An affordable housing application is one submitted to HPD for an MIH or UAP site. The supplied UAP affordable-unit definition includes restriction through an affordable housing regulatory agreement or restrictive declaration and specified income conditions. The additional requirement for UAP sites with 10,000 square feet or more of affordable floor area does not apply based on the stated 6,200 square feet alone.
- UAP and MIH permit and completion-notice procedures: For MIH or UAP developments, HPD may issue a permit notice on or after the restrictive-declaration date, after which DOB may issue permits based on the affordable housing described in the notice. If confirmation of recording is not received within 45 days after the later of the restrictive-declaration date or HPD’s authorization of recording, HPD must suspend or revoke the notice and DOB must suspend or revoke permits issued under it. A permanent certificate of occupancy generally requires an HPD completion notice. HPD may issue that notice only under the stated temporary-certificate-of-occupancy condition for the affordable housing or the stated existing-UAP-site alternative. Developments restricted under an affordable housing regulatory agreement are not required to comply with this section.

ExceptionsNo rear yard equivalent regulations shall apply: to any through lots that extend less than 110 feet in maximum depth from street to street; to large sites; to any zoning lot that includes a through lot portion that is contiguous on one side to two corner lot portions and such zoning lot occupies the entire block frontage of a street; or to any zoning lot occupying an entire block. Subject to the listed exceptions and alternative-location provisions, no: under the ordinary rule for the stated 200-foot-deep through lot, the 100-foot-high portions require a 60-foot-deep rear yard equivalent. The proposed 30-foot open area is 30 feet short for those portions.

Ordinarily, the rear yard equivalent must be midway, or within 10 feet of midway, between the two street lines and unobstructed from its lowest level to the sky, except for permitted obstructions. The supplied provision also allows alternative street-line or side-lot-line layouts, but only for specified eligible-site, tower, R10-without-letter-suffix modification, or qualifying shallow-lot situations. None is established by the stated R7A, 200-foot-deep scenario.

The result does not apply if a stated exception is applicable. The supplied text exempts through lots under 110 feet deep, large sites, certain zoning lots combining a through-lot portion with two corner-lot portions and occupying an entire block frontage, and zoning lots occupying an entire block. The 200-foot depth rules out only the under-110-foot exception on the facts given.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Through-lot definition and interior-lot qualification: A through lot is a zoning lot that is not a corner lot and adjoins two opposite street lines that are parallel, or within 45 degrees of parallel. Any portion that is not, or could not be, bounded by those street lines and two straight lines intersecting them is subject to interior-lot regulations.
- Ordinary depth requirement above 75 feet: For a through lot 190 feet or more in maximum street-to-street depth, a rear yard equivalent must be a 40-foot-deep open area for portions at or below 75 feet and, above 75 feet where permitted, a 60-foot rear yard equivalent. On the stated 200-foot-deep lot, the wings’ portions rising above 75 feet are therefore subject to the 60-foot requirement; a 30-foot area does not meet it.
- Location, open-to-sky condition, alternatives, and exceptions: The standard location is midway, or within 10 feet of midway, between the two street lines, and the rear yard equivalent must be unobstructed from its lowest level to the sky except as provided for permitted obstructions. Specified alternative street-line or side-lot-line layouts are available only to the listed eligible-site, tower, R10-without-letter-suffix modification, or qualifying shallow-lot categories. The provision also states that no rear yard equivalent regulations apply to through lots under 110 feet deep, large sites, the specified combined through-lot/corner-lot configuration occupying an entire block frontage, or zoning lots occupying an entire block.

### Permitext answer

Not based solely on the broker’s statement that the site is close to a subway. The zoning lot’s legally applicable transit geography must be established under the Zoning Resolution’s designated transit-zone boundary and the controlling textual boundary—not by a general proximity description.

If the R6 lot is in the Inner Transit Zone and the units are created after December 5, 2024, no accessory off-street parking is required. For this rule, created means issuance of a temporary certificate of occupancy or, where no temporary certificate has issued, issuance of a final certificate of occupancy. Thus, a planned completion date after December 5, 2024 does not alone establish this condition.

If the lot is in the Outer Transit Zone, and the 60 units are created as part of the development after December 5, 2024, no parking is required for the stated standard market-rate units. The R6 rate is 25 percent, yielding 15 spaces (60 × 0.25), and the waiver threshold is 15 spaces; a result equal to that threshold requires no spaces. Independently, the Outer Transit Zone provision also says no spaces are required where required spaces cannot be arranged with street access conforming to ZR 25-63.

If the lot is beyond the Greater Transit Zone, and the units are created as part of the development after December 5, 2024, the standard R6 rate is 50 percent, or 30 spaces. That exceeds the 10-space waiver threshold, so the table-based waiver does not eliminate parking. But the provision separately states that no spaces are required if required spaces cannot be arranged with street access conforming to ZR 25-63. No facts supplied establish that condition.

So, subway proximity alone does not establish the broker’s conclusion. No parking is established if the lot is confirmed in the Inner Transit Zone with the required post-date creation event, or in the Outer Transit Zone with units created as part of the development after that date. A no-parking result may also arise under the separately stated conforming-street-access rule, including beyond the Greater Transit Zone, if its condition is established.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Transit-zone status is a mapped and text-defined location question: The Greater Transit Zone consists of special parking areas, the Inner Transit Zone, and the Outer Transit Zone, within the boundary designated on ZoLa. The Inner and Outer Transit Zones are designated under ZoLa’s Transit Zones Parking Geographies layer and have stated textual boundary descriptions. Where a textual boundary conflicts with ZoLa, the text controls. A general statement that a site is close to a subway does not establish that the zoning lot is in either transit zone.
- Inner Transit Zone has no parking requirement for newly created units: For R6 and the other listed residence districts within the Inner Transit Zone, no accessory off-street parking is required for dwelling or rooming units created after December 5, 2024. Created means a temporary certificate of occupancy has been issued or, if no temporary certificate has been issued, a final certificate of occupancy has been issued. The separate rule for multiple-dwelling units created between July 20, 1950 and December 5, 2024 does not govern the stated post-date route.
- Outer Transit Zone: 60 standard R6 units meet the table waiver: For units created as part of a development or enlargement after December 5, 2024 in the Outer Transit Zone, the R6 standard-unit rate is 25 percent and the Column C maximum waiver is 15 spaces. No spaces are required where the calculation is less than or equal to Column C. For 60 standard units, the result is 15 spaces, so no spaces are required. Separately, no spaces are required on a zoning lot where there is no way to arrange required spaces with street access conforming to ZR 25-63.
- Beyond the Greater Transit Zone: the R6 table waiver does not cover 60 standard units: For units created as part of a development or enlargement after December 5, 2024 beyond the Greater Transit Zone, the R6 standard-unit rate is 50 percent and the maximum waiver is 10 spaces. For 60 standard units, the calculation is 30 spaces, exceeding the 10-space threshold; therefore, the table-based waiver does not eliminate the parking requirement. Separately, no spaces are required where there is no way to arrange required spaces with street access conforming to ZR 25-63.

### Permitext answer

No. In this Special Mixed Use District, the mixed-use rule does not add the residential and manufacturing FAR allowances. It sets separate use caps, then limits the building’s total floor area to the single use allowance that permits the greatest amount of floor area.

For the 10,000-square-foot zoning lot, qualifying affordable residential floor area in R7A is capped at 5.01 FAR, or 50,100 square feet. The proposed 45,000 square feet is 4.50 FAR and is within that residential cap. M1-4 manufacturing floor area is capped at 2.00 FAR, or 20,000 square feet; the proposed 10,000 square feet is 1.00 FAR and is within that separate use cap.

But the combined proposal is 55,000 square feet: 55,000 ÷ 10,000 = 5.50 FAR. Under the mixed-use rule, the maximum total is the greater individual allowance—here, 5.01 FAR, not 5.01 + 2.00 = 7.01 FAR. The proposed mix therefore exceeds the stated total-floor-area limit by 4,900 square feet (0.49 FAR). ZR 43-12 also expressly says that any given lot area is counted only once in determining FAR.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- R7A qualifying-affordable residential cap: For an R6 through R12 designated Residence District, ZR 123-622 applies the applicable residential floor-area regulations of ZR 23-22. The supplied ZR 23-22 table sets R7A at 5.01 FAR for qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing, distinct from the 4.00 FAR cap for standard residences.
- M1-4 manufacturing cap: ZR 43-12 sets a 2.00 maximum permitted FAR for M1-4. It also states that any given lot area may be counted only once in determining FAR.
- Mixed-use total is not additive: For a zoning lot containing a mixed-use building, ZR 123-623 applies the M1-district cap to manufacturing or commercial uses and the ZR 123-622 residential cap to residential uses. It then provides that the maximum total floor area is the maximum floor area permitted for either the commercial, manufacturing, community-facility, or residential use, whichever permits the greatest amount of floor area. On the stated facts, that is the 5.01 residential FAR allowance, rather than 7.01 FAR.

**Assumptions**

- None

**Missing facts**

- None

**Citations**

- ZR 123-622: Establishes that, for an R6 through R12 designated Residence District, the applicable R6-R12 residential floor-area regulations apply.
- ZR 23-22: Supplies the R7A 5.01 FAR limit for qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing.
- ZR 43-12: Supplies the M1-4 2.00 FAR maximum and the rule that a given lot area is counted only once for FAR.
- ZR 123-623: Controls FAR on a mixed-use building and provides that total floor area is capped by the greatest single applicable use allowance, not the sum of use allowances.

### Expected conclusion

No. The 5.01 residential FAR and 2.00 manufacturing FAR cannot be combined into a 7.01 total FAR. The residential component of 45,000 square feet equals 4.50 FAR and is below the 5.01 residential limit. The manufacturing component of 10,000 square feet equals 1.00 FAR and is below the 2.00 manufacturing limit. However, the total proposal is 55,000 square feet, or 5.50 FAR. The greatest applicable use-specific amount is the qualifying R7A residential amount of 50,100 square feet. The proposed 55,000 square feet exceeds that total mixed-use maximum by 4,900 square feet. Separate component compliance does not cure the total-floor-area violation. (zr.planning.nyc.gov) Special Mixed Use District pairing. Residential FAR drawn from the paired Residence District. Manufacturing FAR drawn from the paired M1 district. Each use must meet its individual cap. Total mixed-use floor area is limited to the greatest applicable amount. FARs are not stacked. Qualifying affordable housing affects the residential amount, not the manufacturing amount. Residential FAR: 45,000 sf ÷ 10,000 sf = 4.50 FAR Manufacturing FAR: 10,000 sf ÷ 10,000 sf = 1.00 FAR Total FAR: 55,000 sf ÷ 10,000 sf = 5.50 FAR Maximum residential amount: 10,000 sf × 5.01 = 50,100 sf Maximum manufacturing amount: 10,000 sf × 2.00 = 20,000 sf Controlling total amount: Greater of 50,100 sf and 20,000 sf = 50,100 sf Excess total floor area: 55,000 sf − 50,100 sf = 4,900 sf

**Question:** A lawful 20,000-square-foot warehouse has operated continuously in an R6B district since 1980. It occupied the same 20,000 square feet on December 17, 1987, and has never been enlarged. We want to add 5,000 square feet. Aren’t nonconforming uses allowed a 25 percent enlargement?

### Selected evidence

**ZR 52-11 — canonical section 20018626**

> A non-conforming use may be continued, except as otherwise provided in this Chapter.

**ZR 52-41 — canonical section 20018642**

> A non-conforming use may be enlarged or extended within the district in which such non-conforming use is located only in accordance with the provisions of this Chapter. However, a non-conforming single- or two-family residence in an R3, R4 or R5 District may be enlarged or extended in accordance with the bulk regulations specified for the district in which it is located. Furthermore, enlargements or extensions designed exclusively to permit conformity with the regulations on performance standards, designed in order to improve energy performance, or designed in order to provide required accessory off-street parking spaces or off-street loading berths on the same zoning lot as the use to which such spaces or berths are accessory are not subject to the restrictions set forth herein.

For the purposes of this Section and Sections 52-31 (General Provisions) and 52-42 (C6, C8 or Manufacturing Districts), the applicable district regulations on accessory off-street parking spaces or loading berths shall be determined in accordance with the following tables. The term "required" as used in this Section shall mean some or all of, but not more than, the number of spaces or berths which would be required or permitted by such applicable district regulations for development for such use.

For non-conforming use in Residence Districts, accessory off-street parking spaces or loading berths shall be subject to the provisions of Sections 25-65 or 25-77 (Screening).

In the Manhattan Core, enlargements or extensions of non-conforming uses which involve the provision of off-street parking are subject to the regulations set forth in Article I, Chapter 3, and in the Long Island City area, as defined in Section 16-02 (Definitions), such enlargements or extensions are subject to the regulations set forth in Article I, Chapter 6.

In the case of a conflict between these provisions and retail continuity provisions that apply to the ground floor of buildings, a non-conforming use on the ground floor in such building may be changed only to a conforming use.

**ZR 52-42 — canonical section 20018643**

> Except for the use of land with minor improvements, in Commercial Districts or Manufacturing Districts, a use which is non-conforming with respect to the applicable district regulations on performance standards, may be enlarged or extended, provided that:

such enlarged or extended portion does not occupy more than 25 percent of the floor area or space which such non-conforming use occupied or utilized within the building or other structure at the time when it became non-conforming; provided, however, that in no event shall any such enlargement or extension create a non-compliance or increase the degree of non-compliance of a non-complying building or other structure; such enlarged or extended portion conforms to the applicable district regulations on performance standards and on accessory off-street parking spaces and loading berths, as set forth in Section 52-41 (General Provisions); and such non-conformance with the performance standards was not self-created.

In the specified districts, such use may be extended into any floor area where it would be permitted as a changed use under the provisions of Section 52-35 (Manufacturing or Related Uses in Commercial Districts), provided that the applicable district regulations on performance standards and accessory off-street loading berths, as set forth in Section 52-41 (General Provisions), shall apply to such extended floor area.

**ZR 52-43 — canonical section 20018645**

> In all Residence Districts, except R1 and R2 Districts, a fire station may be enlarged or extended, provided that:

(a) such enlarged or extended portion does not occupy more than 25 percent of the floor area or space which such non-conforming use occupied or utilized within the building or other structure at the time when it became non-conforming;

(b) such enlargement or extension shall not create a non-compliance or increase the degree of non-compliance; and

(c) such enlarged or extended portion conforms to the applicable district regulations on accessory off-street parking spaces as set forth in Section 52-41 (General Provisions).

Community facility bulk regulations as set forth in Article II, Chapter 4, shall apply to such enlarged or extended fire stations.

**ZR 73-53 — canonical section 20018870**

> (a) In all districts, the Board of Standards and Appeals may modify use and bulk regulations to permit the enlargement or extension of floor area of a conforming or non-conforming referenced commercial and manufacturing use, provided that:

(1) such use is not subject to termination pursuant to Section 52-70, et seq.;

(2) the use for which such special permit is being sought has been lawfully located on the zoning lot on which the expansion is to occur, or a portion thereof, for five years or more;

(3) the building in which such use is located has not previously been enlarged, pursuant to Sections 11-412, 43-121 or 72-21;

(4) the use is not one listed in Use Group X and is permitted only in Manufacturing Districts; and

(5) in a Residence District, such enlargement or extension shall be permitted in existing floor area or on a vacant portion of a zoning lot only when no lawful residential use has occupied such floor area or vacant portion of a zoning lot at any time during the five years prior to the date of application for such special permit.

(b) Any enlargement or extension permitted pursuant to this Section shall be subject to the following requirements:

(1) the permitted enlargement or extension may be the greater of:

(i) 45 percent of the floor area occupied by such use on December 17, 1987; or

(ii) 2,500 square feet additional to the floor area occupied by such use on December 17, 1987.

In no event shall the amount of enlargement or extension under paragraph (b)(i) of this Section exceed 10,000 square feet additional to the floor area occupied by such use on December 17, 1987;

(2) unless the zoning lot is located within an M2 or M3 District, more than 300 feet from a Residence District boundary, any enlarged or extended portion, or activity generated by such enlargement or extension, including storage and processing, shall be in completely enclosed buildings;

(3) in the case of a non-conforming use, such enlarged or extended use shall conform to all performance standards applicable in an M1 District located at the boundary with a Residence District; however, conforming uses shall conform to the applicable performance standards of the district in which they are located;

(4) no open uses of any kind, including storage or processing, shall be permitted within 30 feet of a rear lot line that is located within a Residence District or within 30 feet of the rear lot line that coincides with a rear lot line of a zoning lot in a Residence District;

(5) no enlargement or extension that exceeds 16 feet above curb level shall be permitted within 30 feet of the rear lot line that coincides with a rear lot line of a zoning lot in a Residence District;

(6) no enlargement or extension that exceeds 16 feet above curb level shall be permitted within eight feet of the side lot line that coincides with a rear lot line of a zoning lot in a Residence District;

(7) no open uses of any kind, including storage or processing, shall be permitted within eight feet of the side lot line that coincides with a rear lot line of a zoning lot in a Residence District;

(8) no enlargement or extension, or open uses of any kind, including storage or processing, shall be permitted within eight feet of the lot line which coincides with a side lot line of a zoning lot in an R1, R2, R3, R4 or R5 District; and

(9) no side yard shall be required in an R6 through R12 Districts or in a Commercial District or Manufacturing District; however, if such side yard is provided, it must be at least eight feet in width.

(c) In granting such special permit, the Board shall find:

(1) that such enlargement or extension will not generate significant increases in vehicular or pedestrian traffic nor cause congestion in the surrounding area;

(2) that there will be adequate parking for any vehicles generated by such enlargement or extension;

(3) that any required side yard shall be suitably landscaped or fenced as the Board shall prescribe;

(4) that any accessory parking or loading generated by such enlargement or extension shall be suitably buffered from adjacent uses by methods that the Board shall prescribe; and

(5) that the special permit, if granted, will not alter the essential character of the neighborhood or district in which the use is located, nor impair the future use or development of the surrounding area.

The Board may prescribe appropriate conditions and safeguards including, if appropriate, limitations on hours of parking and delivery, requirements for off-street loading, and location of curb cuts to minimize adverse effects of the enlargement, extension or existing uses on the character of the surrounding area, and to protect residential or commercial zoning lots.

### Permitext answer

No—not as an as-of-right 25 percent enlargement in an R6B district. A non-conforming use may be continued, but enlargement is allowed only where Chapter 5 provides it. The 25 percent rule in ZR 52-42 applies in Commercial or Manufacturing Districts to a use non-conforming as to applicable performance standards; it does not create a 25 percent enlargement right for this warehouse in a Residence District. The supplied Residence District 25 percent rule applies to fire stations, not warehouses.

The proposed increase is 5,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf = 25 percent. Although that equals the 25 percent cap in those provisions, the numerical match does not create an entitlement.

The supplied potential route is a BSA special permit under ZR 73-53, which applies in all districts to a qualifying referenced commercial and manufacturing use. Your facts establish two stated threshold items: the use has been lawfully located on the zoning lot for more than five years, and the building has not previously been enlarged under the three sections listed in ZR 73-53(a)(3). If the warehouse also meets the remaining eligibility conditions, the applicable size allowance is the greater of 45 percent of its December 17, 1987 floor area or 2,500 sf. The calculation is 20,000 sf × 45% = 9,000 sf; 9,000 sf is greater than 2,500 sf and is below the 10,000-sf cap. Thus, 5,000 sf is within the special-permit numerical maximum, but it is not authorized unless BSA grants the special permit.

For a Residence District, that route permits the addition only in existing floor area or on a vacant portion of the zoning lot where no lawful residential use occupied that specific area at any time in the five years before the application. The special-permit conditions also apply as triggered: because the stated site is in R6B, the rule requiring the enlarged/extended portion and activity it generates, including storage and processing, to be in completely enclosed buildings applies; its exception is only for a zoning lot in an M2 or M3 District more than 300 feet from a Residence District boundary. The enlarged non-conforming use must meet the specified M1-at-a-Residence-District-boundary performance standards. Lot-line, open-use, and height restrictions depend on the actual relationship of the proposed work to the particular rear and side lot lines described in the provision. In R6 through R12 Districts, no side yard is required, but any side yard provided must be at least eight feet wide. BSA must also make the listed traffic, parking, side-yard treatment where applicable, buffering, and neighborhood-character findings.

ZR 52-41 separately provides that a non-conforming use may be enlarged only under Chapter 5, while excluding work designed exclusively for performance-standard conformity, energy-performance improvement, or required accessory parking/loading on the same zoning lot from its restrictions. The stated 5,000-sf warehouse addition is not established as one of those exclusively designed projects.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Continuation does not authorize enlargement: A non-conforming use may be continued, except as otherwise provided in Chapter 5. That continuation rule does not independently authorize enlargement.
- Enlargement is governed by Chapter 5: A non-conforming use may be enlarged or extended only in accordance with Chapter 5. The stated restrictions do not apply to work designed exclusively for performance-standard conformity, energy-performance improvement, or required accessory parking or loading on the same zoning lot. The proposed warehouse addition is not established as falling within one of those exclusive-purpose exclusions.
- The commercial/manufacturing-district 25 percent rule does not apply in R6B: ZR 52-42 permits a performance-standard non-conforming use to be enlarged or extended in Commercial or Manufacturing Districts, subject to a 25 percent cap based on the area occupied when it became non-conforming, no new or increased building non-compliance, conformity of the enlarged portion with applicable performance, parking, and loading regulations, and a non-self-created performance-standard non-conformance. The stated R6B location is a Residence District, so this provision does not establish a warehouse enlargement right.
- The Residence District 25 percent provision is confined to fire stations: In Residence Districts other than R1 and R2, ZR 52-43 permits enlargement or extension of a fire station subject to its 25 percent cap, no new or increased non-compliance, applicable accessory parking requirements, and community-facility bulk regulations. It does not authorize a warehouse enlargement.
- A BSA special permit can provide a larger conditional allowance: ZR 73-53 permits BSA, in all districts, to modify use and bulk regulations for enlargement of floor area of a qualifying referenced commercial and manufacturing use, subject to its eligibility criteria, Residence District location limitation, physical requirements, and findings. The stated facts establish lawful location for more than five years and no prior enlargement under the three provisions named in ZR 73-53(a)(3). For a qualifying use, the allowance is the greater of 45 percent of December 17, 1987 floor area or 2,500 square feet, with the percentage alternative capped at 10,000 square feet. At 20,000 square feet on that date, 45 percent is 9,000 square feet; the proposed 5,000 square feet is within that numerical maximum, but only a granted special permit can authorize it.

**Assumptions**

- None

**Missing facts**

- Whether the warehouse is a qualifying referenced commercial and manufacturing use, is not listed in Use Group X, and is permitted only in Manufacturing Districts, as ZR 73-53(a)(4) requires.
- Whether the use is subject to termination under ZR 52-70 et seq.
- Whether the proposed 5,000 square feet would be in existing floor area or on a vacant portion of the zoning lot, and whether any lawful residential use occupied that specific area during the five years before the special-permit application.
- The proposed addition’s location, height above curb level, relationship to relevant rear and side lot lines, and any proposed open storage or processing, which determine whether the conditional lot-line, open-use, and height restrictions apply.
- Whether the enlarged non-conforming use will conform to the M1-at-a-Residence-District-boundary performance standards specified by ZR 73-53.
- The parking generated by the enlargement and the proposed accessory parking or loading arrangement, needed for BSA’s required parking-adequacy and buffering findings.
- Whether BSA can make the required findings on traffic/congestion, required side-yard treatment if any side yard is required, parking/loading buffering, and neighborhood character and future development.

**Citations**

- ZR 52-11: Establishes that a non-conforming use may be continued, subject to Chapter 5.
- ZR 52-41: Establishes that enlargement is governed by Chapter 5 and preserves the stated exclusive-purpose exclusions.
- ZR 52-42: Limits the supplied 25 percent performance-standard enlargement rule to Commercial and Manufacturing Districts.
- ZR 52-43: Limits the supplied Residence District 25 percent enlargement rule to fire stations.
- ZR 73-53: Establishes the conditional BSA special-permit route, size calculation, Residence District condition, triggered physical restrictions, and required findings.

### Expected conclusion

No automatic 25 percent enlargement has been established. The cited 25 percent rule in ZR §52-42 does not generally apply to a warehouse in an R6B district, and the Residence District provision in ZR §52-43 concerns fire stations. A BSA special permit under ZR §73-53 may be a possible path if the exact warehouse use is eligible and all conditions and findings are satisfied. Using the supplied December 17, 1987 floor area, the potential numerical enlargement limit would be 9,000 square feet because 45 percent of 20,000 square feet is 9,000 square feet, which is greater than 2,500 square feet and below the 10,000-square-foot cap. The proposed 5,000 square feet is below that numerical ceiling, but that does not make it permitted as-of-right or guarantee a BSA approval. **Question:** Our 20,000-square-foot zoning lot is split by a district boundary: 8,000 square feet is in R7A and 12,000 square feet is in R8A. We want 110,000 square feet of standard market-rate residential floor area and plan to put most of the building on the R8A side. Can we use the R8A FAR for the whole lot?

### Selected evidence

**ZR 23-22 — canonical section 20018017**

> R6 R7 R8 R9 R10 R11 R12

In the districts indicated, the maximum residential floor area ratio shall be as set forth in the following table. Separate maximum residential floor area ratios are set forth for zoning lots containing standard residences and zoning lots containing qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing.

MAXIMUM FLOOR AREA RATIO FOR R6-R12 DISTRICTS

District Standard residences Qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing R6A R61 R6-1 R7B 3.00 3.90 R6 2.20 3.90 R6B 2.00 2.40 R6D R6-2 2.50 3.00 R7A R7-11 R7-21 4.00 5.01 R7-1 R7-2 3.44 5.01 R7D 4.66 5.60 R7X R7-3 5.00 6.00 R8A R8X R8 6.02 7.20 R8 7.201 8.642 R8B 4.00 4.80 R9A R9 7.52 9.02 R9D R9X R9-1 9.00 10.80 R10A R10X R10 10.00 12.00 R11 12.00 15.00 R12 15.00 18.00

1 For zoning lots, or portions thereof, located within 100 feet of a wide street

2 Outside of Mandatory Inclusionary Housing areas, for zoning lots, or portions thereof, located within 100 feet of a wide street, containing UAP developments or qualifying senior housing

**ZR 77-02 — canonical section 20018891**

> Whenever a zoning lot is divided by a boundary between two or more districts and such zoning lot did not exist on December 15, 1961, or any applicable subsequent amendment thereto, each portion of such zoning lot shall be regulated by all the provisions applicable to the district in which such portion of the zoning lot is located. However, the provisions of Section 77-22 (Floor Area Ratio) shall apply to zoning lots created at any time where different bulk regulations apply to different portions of such zoning lot.

**ZR 77-22 — canonical section 20018899**

> The maximum floor area ratio permitted on each portion of such zoning lot for the applicable type of building or buildings on such zoning lot shall be determined under the applicable regulations of this Resolution.

Each such floor area ratio shall be multiplied by the percentage of the zoning lot to which such floor area ratio applies. The sum of the products thus obtained shall be the adjusted maximum floor area ratio applicable to such zoning lot.

The floor area resulting from application of the adjusted maximum floor area ratio may be located anywhere on the zoning lot, subject to all other regulations of this Resolution, and provided that the floor area ratio for any portion of the zoning lot within one district shall not exceed the maximum floor area ratio, specified for that district, or the adjusted maximum floor area ratio for the zoning lot, whichever is greater, except that the portion of the zoning lot fronting on and within 100 feet of a wide street and permitting the greater maximum permitted residential floor area ratio may exceed the maximum permitted residential floor area ratio for the portion of the zoning lot by up to 20 percent.

In applying the provisions of this Section, the following conditions shall apply:

the floor area bonus permitted for publicly accessible open areas or arcades, under the applicable regulations of this Resolution, shall apply only to such publicly accessible open areas, arcades or portions thereof, as are located in a district in which such bonus is granted; and when a zoning lot contains a sky exposure plane building which does not have a specified maximum floor area ratio, for the purpose of computing the adjusted maximum floor area ratio, the floor area ratio of such zoning lot shall be deemed to be that which can be achieved at the minimum required open space ratio for such zoning lot.

### Permitext answer

No. You cannot apply the R8A standard-residence FAR to the entire 20,000-square-foot zoning lot merely because most of the building would be on the R8A side. The supplied table gives standard residences a 4.00 FAR in R7A and 6.02 FAR in R8A. For a zoning lot with portions subject to different bulk regulations, ZR 77-22 requires an area-weighted adjusted FAR, while allowing floor area produced by that adjusted FAR to be located anywhere on the lot subject to the stated portion limits and other Resolution regulations.

Using the stated areas, the adjusted FAR is: (8,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf × 4.00) + (12,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf × 6.02) = 5.212. The corresponding baseline floor area is 20,000 sf × 5.212 = 104,240 sf. Your proposed 110,000 sf equals 5.50 FAR, not 6.02 FAR.

Absent the stated wide-street exception, 104,240 sf is the lot-wide maximum under this calculation, and 110,000 sf is 5,760 sf above it. ZR 77-22 contains a separate exception: the portion that both fronts on and is within 100 feet of a wide street, and permits the greater maximum permitted residential FAR, may exceed that portion’s maximum residential FAR by up to 20 percent. The selected evidence does not establish whether that exception applies here, so it does not establish that 110,000 sf is permitted. It also does not change the conclusion that the R8A FAR cannot simply be used across the full lot.

Without the exception, the R8A portion may not exceed 6.02 FAR because that is greater than the 5.212 adjusted lot FAR. If the exception applies, the stated 20-percent ceiling would be 7.224 FAR for the qualifying R8A portion; that is a portion-location allowance, not permission in the supplied text to treat all 20,000 square feet as R8A.

**What the selected evidence establishes**

- Standard-residence FARs differ by district: For zoning lots containing standard residences, the supplied table specifies a 4.00 maximum residential FAR in R7A and a 6.02 maximum residential FAR in R8A. The separate column for qualifying affordable housing or qualifying senior housing does not apply to the stated standard market-rate residential proposal.
- A split zoning lot uses an adjusted FAR: Where different bulk regulations apply to different portions of a zoning lot, ZR 77-02 provides that ZR 77-22 applies to zoning lots created at any time. ZR 77-22 requires the applicable FAR for each portion to be multiplied by that portion’s percentage of the zoning lot, with the sum becoming the adjusted maximum FAR applicable to the zoning lot.
- Floor area can be concentrated subject to portion caps and the wide-street exception: Floor area resulting from the adjusted maximum FAR may be located anywhere on the zoning lot, subject to all other Resolution regulations. Ordinarily, the FAR in any district portion may not exceed the greater of that district’s specified maximum FAR or the adjusted lot FAR. However, the portion fronting on and within 100 feet of a wide street, and permitting the greater maximum permitted residential FAR, may exceed that portion’s maximum permitted residential FAR by up to 20 percent.

**Assumptions**

- None

**Missing facts**

- Whether the R8A portion that permits the greater residential FAR fronts on and is within 100 feet of a wide street, such that the express ZR 77-22 wide-street exception may apply.

**Citations**

- ZR 23-22: Supplies the R7A and R8A standard-residence FAR values.
- ZR 77-02: Makes ZR 77-22 applicable to a zoning lot with portions subject to different bulk regulations.
- ZR 77-22: Supplies the adjusted-FAR method, floor-area location rule, portion cap, and wide-street exception.

### Expected conclusion

No. The R8A FAR cannot simply be applied to the entire zoning lot. The lot requires a weighted calculation under ZR §77-22. Forty percent of the lot is in R7A and 60 percent is in R8A. The adjusted standard residential FAR is therefore 5.212, producing a maximum of 104,240 square feet. The proposed 110,000 square feet equals 5.50 FAR and exceeds the adjusted maximum by 5,760 square feet. ZR §77-22 may allow resulting floor area to be distributed on the zoning lot, subject to its limits and all other district rules, but moving more of the building to the R8A portion does not increase the calculated total floor area. Per-portion limitations and other bulk rules remain applicable. No use of the higher FAR across the entire lot. R7A portion: 8,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf = 40% R8A portion: 12,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf = 60% Weighted R7A contribution: 4.00 × 40% = 1.600 FAR Weighted R8A contribution: 6.02 × 60% = 3.612 FAR Adjusted maximum FAR: 1.600 + 3.612 = 5.212 FAR Maximum floor area: 20,000 sf × 5.212 = 104,240 sf Proposed FAR: 110,000 sf ÷ 20,000 sf = 5.50 FAR Excess: 110,000 sf − 104,240 sf = 5,760 sf
